# Role Configuration

Roles are project responsibilities. Agent bindings are configurable defaults.

## Default Bindings

| Role | Default agent | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Architect | Codex; scoped Claude exception for the v6.9 site mock-up work on `arch/canonical-v69-pivot` (2026-10-06; see below) | Creates feature maps, causal sequencing, protected seams, and feature-to-task guidance. |
| Planner | Codex (Claude exception ended at the 2026-10-03 21:23 reset; see below) | Converts reviewed feature guidance into small UI-verifiable task definitions. |
| Implementer | Claude | Builds the active task, runs checks, and prepares the review packet. |
| Reviewer | Configurable independent agent; Codex by default (see below) | Reviews work independently from the author. |

## Rules

- Keep role responsibilities stable even if the bound agent changes.
- Do not assume Planner, Architect, or Implementer means a specific vendor unless
  this file says so.
- Implementer may refine implementation steps, but should not redefine feature
  sequencing without Architect or user review.
- Reviewer must be independent from the agent that authored the implementation.
- Claude Code commands in `.claude/commands/` should read this file before
  acting so they can either run the role or prepare a handoff for the configured
  agent.

## Decision Requests Go To The Active Architect

Set by the owner on 2026-10-04 (verbatim in `.agent/owner-T028-closeout-2026-10-04.md`):
"for all decsion requests, let the active architect make them and inform me if
the architect wants me to weigh in as well with its justification and decision".

So the coordinator does not put decision requests to the owner. It sends them
to the Architect bound above, which decides, records the decision with its
justification, and informs the owner. Where the Architect wants the owner to
weigh in, it says so, giving its decision and justification, and the owner may
confirm or overrule. Owner acceptance of a slice (USER_REVIEW_REQUIRED) is not
a decision request and still goes to the owner.

## Scoped Claude Architect Exception: v6.9 Site Mock-ups (2026-10-06)

The owner set Architect to Claude for one piece of work: comparing the site
pages of the static mock-ups in `frontend/mockups/v69/` with v6.9 sections
8.18-8.26 (mini-grid), 8.28-8.31 (cold chain), 8.35 (readiness) and 8.36
(financials), and **updating the mock-up screens** with the enhanced version.
Owner text verbatim: `.agent/owner-v69-site-mockup-comparison-2026-10-06.md`.

- Extended by the owner the same day (follow-up 2 in the same file): EV
  recommendations, interactive site selection across mini-grid, cold chain
  and EV, a date-range selection bar, and the site single line diagram.
- Extended again (follow-ups 3-6 in the same file): beyond the site pages to
  the global Assets (incl. warranty/vendor economics, registration/edit),
  Findings, Evidence, Work (Actions, Incidents, Maintenance, Operational
  records) and a limited-demo Settings area of the mock-ups, and site
  creation (form, template, batch upload; follow-up 8).
- Scope: branch `arch/canonical-v69-pivot`, mock-up files only. Backend,
  frontend app, gateway and simulator realization planning stay Codex-bound.
- For this scope the Architect also edits the mock-up files directly (owner
  instruction); the usual Architect/Implementer split does not apply to them.
- Outputs state they were produced by Claude under this exception.
- Revert this row to plain Codex when the owner accepts the enhanced mock-ups
  or says so.

Revert all three rows in the table above when the reset has passed. Until then,
every Architect pass, Planner task definition and review report states it was produced by Claude under
this exception, so a later reader can decide whether a judgement should go back

review, codex will review what is available to review when it returns." Every

## Architect Was Temporarily Claude, And Is Codex Again

On 2026-09-24 the user set Architect to Claude because Codex had exhausted its
usage limit twice in one day while the task queue waited on an Architect
review. That entry said to revert when capacity allowed. Codex capacity
returned at 18:21 the same day and the binding was reverted at 18:53, so the
exception is closed and the rule below applies again without qualification.

Two Architect passes were produced under the exception, both on
`arch/queue-review-feedback` and both merged: the queue resequencing at
`2970d74` and the hardening-gate removal at `e02d267`. A later reader checking
who authored them should know they were Claude by an explicit, recorded
decision rather than by the coordinator routing around this file.

The lesson worth keeping is the one the exception was written to respect: when
a binding has to change, change it here first and then act. Routing around a
control is worse than the mistake the control prevents.

## Invoking A Codex-Bound Role

A role bound to Codex is run by invoking Codex, not by dispatching the
same-named Claude subagent and merging what it returns. Codex is on PATH and
runs non-interactively:

```
codex exec "<the role brief>"
```

**The obligation is the coordinator's, not the subagent's.** On 2026-09-23 the
Architect correctly flagged three times that it was producing Architect output
while this file bound the role to Codex. Each time the coordinator relayed the
flag to the user as a question and merged the output anyway. A subagent noticing
a binding violation is not a control, because it has already done the work by
the time it can tell anyone. The control is that the coordinator invokes the
bound tool in the first place.

So: before dispatching `assetops-architect` or `assetops-planner`, check the
binding above. If it is Codex, run `codex exec`. The Claude subagents of those
names refuse Codex-bound work rather than producing it.

## The Reviewer Binding, And Its Backup

The Reviewer is **Codex by default**. When Codex is unavailable - usage limit,
outage, or a reset hours away - the backup is a **fresh Claude
`assetops-reviewer` subagent**, and the substitution needs no permission. A
slice waiting a day for quota is worse than a slice reviewed by the weaker of
two independent readers.

**Independence is the rule the backup must not break.** The backup is a NEW
agent instance. It is never the agent that implemented the slice, and never
that agent resumed with a different instruction. An implementer asked to
review its own work will confirm it, which is why the binding exists.

State in the report which reviewer produced it. A Claude review of Claude
implementation is genuinely weaker than a Codex one on the same slice, and a
later reader deciding how much weight to give a verdict needs to know which
they are reading. Where the substitution happened because of quota, say so and
say what was left unjudged, so the question can go back to Codex if it matters
enough to wait for.

## Which Reviewer For Which Round

Set by the user on 2026-09-26 to conserve Codex quota, which has been the
binding constraint on this milestone three times. This is not the same as the
backup rule above: it applies whether or not Codex is available.

**Codex reviews discovery.** The first review of any slice, a contract version
move, a new mechanism or a restructure, and any round where the Implementer had
to choose between legitimate treatments. T020B's R1 was that round - state a
rule for unaligned windows or refuse them at setup - and it needed the stronger
reader.

**A fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` reviews verification.** A correction round
where an independent reviewer has already named the defects and the fix needs
no design choice: prose, docstrings, claim corrections, test assertions,
deletions. The job is confirming the named items closed and nothing else moved.
Fresh rather than resumed, because there is no context worth preserving for
checking named sentences and a clean reader is worth more.

The honest risk, recorded so it is not discovered later: this relies on the
previous review having found everything discoverable in that area. Four
consecutive rounds on T020B each found something the last missed, which is the
argument against cheap re-reviews. **So a Claude verification pass that finds
something unexpected is a signal to escalate that round back to Codex, not a
cheap verdict to accept.** Finding an unnamed defect means the round was
discovery after all and was misclassified.

Two classification traps worth naming, both seen on T020B:

- **A correction round can widen under the Implementer's hand.** The R1a/R1b
  round was three sentences and a deletion when dispatched; it came back having
  also corrected an unnamed fourth sentence and added a property-based guard
  over thirteen windows. Classify on what came back, not on what was sent.
- **"Small" will drift to "everything" if it is not tested against the two
  lists above.** The question is not how many lines changed. It is whether a
  reader had to decide anything.

## Owner Review Routing From S3 Onward (2026-10-11)

Owner (verbatim, `.agent/owner-review-routing-and-merge-2026-10-11.md`): "codex usage optimizaion is important, so keep the same arragment for S2 going forward". For every slice from S3 onward: a fresh Claude `assetops-reviewer` does the slice's first review and every re-review; small written-list fix rounds run on a Sonnet implementer; one batched Codex review covers each milestone's slices together at the milestone's end; Codex Architect/Planner questions are batched rather than sent per question file. This overrides "Codex reviews discovery" below for per-slice first reviews; Codex remains the Architect, the Planner and the milestone reviewer.

## Do Not Write The Review In The Brief

Recorded on 2026-09-26 at the user's instruction, because this project had
been doing the opposite. Reviewer briefs had grown to eight numbered questions,
each naming the thing to judge and supplying the arguments on both sides. That
is not a brief; it is a draft verdict with blanks. A reviewer steered to eight
questions inspects eight things, and the ninth - the one nobody thought to ask
about - is the one that reaches production.

A brief gives the Reviewer:

- the target: branch, commit, spec, packet, and where prior reviews live;
- the standing criteria it is reviewing against, which live in the task file
  and this project's records, not in the brief;
- **what has already been run and verified**, with results, so quota is not
  spent re-running a green suite. This is factual and does not steer;
- the constraints: do not merge, do not fix, where to write, what not to touch.

A brief does **not** give the Reviewer:

- an enumerated list of what to judge;
- the arguments for or against a conclusion;
- which findings the coordinator considers important;
- a named preferred outcome, or a hint of one.

Pointing at an artifact is not steering; summarising what that artifact says is.
Where a genuine question must be asked - a decision only the user can take, a
constraint the Reviewer cannot infer - ask it plainly and separately, and do not
dress an opinion as a question.

The Implementer's packet already states its own uncertainties and residual
risks. That is the right channel for "look at this": the author declaring what
it is unsure of, in a document the Reviewer reads anyway.

## Sort Findings Before Forwarding Them

Recorded on 2026-09-26 after the user asked whether the Reviewer was applying
`D-2026-09-22-milestone-speed-over-purity`. It was; all three T020A1 reviews
cited it, and Codex used it to carry an HTTP-reachable 500, five overflow
sites and several residuals rather than block on them. **The coordinator was
the leak.**

That decision's stopping rule is narrow. Stop only for a defect expensive to
reverse, a claim that would mislead an Implementer, or a decision only the user
can take. **Everything else is recorded and carried.** A Reviewer that rates a
finding low and does not demand it has already applied the rule; forwarding it
as fix work overrides the Reviewer in the direction the decision forbids.

So when a review returns, the coordinator sorts every finding into block or
carry against those three conditions before putting any of it to the user, and
says which is which. A finding that is carried goes to
`.ai/MILESTONE_REVIEW_BACKLOG.md` with the reason, not into the next round.

T020A1 is the case to remember: five implementation rounds and three review
passes, of which two rounds closed comment counts, a docstring and a Draft
count that had drifted. Each was real. None was a reason to reopen a slice.

## Using Codex Efficiently

Codex quota is **account-level and shared across every model**. On 2026-09-24
it was exhausted twice in one day, the second time mid-review, and switching
to `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-sol` and `gpt-5.5` all returned the
identical limit with the identical reset time. **You cannot route around an
exhausted limit by changing model.** The only lever is spending less per call,
before the limit is reached.

What it cost that day, measured from the run logs:

| Pass | Tokens | Shell calls | Started |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Reviewer pass 1 | 145,247 | 31 | cold |
| Reviewer pass 2 | 145,568 | 38 | cold |
| Reviewer pass 3 | 25,259, then died | 5 | cold |

Pass 2 spent 145k tokens re-deriving what pass 1 had already established.

### Resume instead of restarting

`codex exec resume <session-id> "<follow-up prompt>"` continues an existing
session with its context intact. `--last` resumes the most recent. The session
id is printed in the header of every run, so **capture it whenever a Codex
role may be returned to** - which is every review, because a review that finds
defects is returned to by definition.

A second pass on the same slice is a follow-up, not a new engagement. Resume
it and say what changed. Start cold only when the subject is genuinely
different or the earlier session would mislead.

**`resume` does not take the same flags as `exec`, and this cost two failed
invocations on 2026-09-25 before it was pinned down.** Two rules:

- Options go **before** the session id: `codex exec resume [OPTIONS]
  <session-id> "<prompt>"`. After it they are parsed as the prompt.
- **`--sandbox` does not exist on `resume`.** Pass it as config instead:
  `-c sandbox_mode="workspace-write"`. `resume` accepts `-c`, `--last`,
  `--all`, `-m`, `--skip-git-repo-check`, `--json`, `-o` and a few others;
  run `codex exec resume --help` rather than assuming a flag carries over.

The working form, verified:

```
codex exec resume -c sandbox_mode="workspace-write" --skip-git-repo-check   <session-id> "<follow-up prompt>" < /dev/null
```

Both failures were argument parsing, rejected before any model call, so they
cost seconds and no quota. The point of recording them is that guidance which
fails on first use is worse than none.

A session resumes across a quota interruption: the third-pass review that died
mid-run at its usage limit was resumed the next day into the same session id
with its context intact.

### Match the model to the work

`-m <model>` selects one. `gpt-6-astra` is the default and the most expensive;
`gpt-6-sol` is the workhorse coding model; `gpt-6-luna` is the cheap one. Full
list: `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`, `gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra`,
`gpt-5.6-luna`, `gpt-5.5`.

Reserve Astra for genuine architecture and review judgement. Mechanical passes
- a fix round against a written list, a routine Planner expansion, a
consistency sweep - do not need it.

### Never spend a full session on a probe

`~/.codex/config.toml` sets `model_reasoning_effort = "high"` globally, while
the models' own default is `low`. A one-line availability probe therefore runs
at high reasoning. If you must probe, use
`-m gpt-6-luna -c model_reasoning_effort=low`. Better: attempt the real work
and detect the limit from the error, which costs nothing when the limit is
already hit.

### Put verification in the brief, not in the session

Every fact the brief states is work the session does not repeat. Say what you
already ran and what you already verified - test counts, check results,
confirmed line numbers - and say plainly that repeating it is not wanted.
Ask Codex for judgement, not for re-running a green suite.

### Invocation mechanics that have already gone wrong

- **Pass the prompt as an argument, never on stdin.** `codex exec ... - < file`
  blocked for three hours with no output on 2026-09-23. Always redirect
  `< /dev/null`.
- **Do not double-background.** `nohup codex ... &` inside an already
  backgrounded call returns instantly, and the harness reports a completion
  that has not happened. Background the call once.
- **A branch carries a snapshot of these rules.** A session started on a task
  branch reads that branch's `.ai/`, not main's. Merge main into the branch
  before a review if a rule has changed.

## Using The Claude Implementer Efficiently

Set by the owner on 2026-09-29 after T023, T022B and T024 each cost 80-200M
re-read tokens and one to three hours. Measured from the transcripts: cost is
context size times turns, and context grew from resumed agents and from source
code read whole into context. The agent's own rules are in
`.claude/agents/assetops-implementer.md` "Context budget"; these are the
coordinator's:

- **A fresh implementer per build and per fix round. Never resume one.**
  T024's build started at 209k tokens because the read-only planning agent was
  resumed; T023's fix rounds started at 458k. If a slice needs a planning pass,
  the planner writes its plan to `.agent/T0NN-implementer-plan.md` and a fresh
  agent builds from that file.
- **Fix rounds run on Sonnet** (`model: sonnet` on dispatch), briefed with the
  review file and the findings to fix. New slices stay on the session model,
  where design judgement matters.
- **Keep slices small.** A task the implementer sizes at several times a
  previous slice is a split candidate before it is built, not after.
- **Build large slices in phases, a fresh agent per phase.** Set by the owner
  on 2026-09-29 after T025a: bounded reads cut read volume by about two thirds,
  but 508 turns still took context to 467k and cost 142M re-read tokens,
  because cost grows with the square of the turn count. A slice beyond one
  focused change is dispatched as phases (for example contracts and core, then
  model and setup, then Lab and frontend). Each phase ends with committed work
  and a short handoff at `.agent/T0NN-phase-N-handoff.md` (what landed, file
  anchors, what is next, open risks); the coordinator dispatches a fresh agent
  for the next phase from that file. Full suites and the packet belong to the
  last phase.
- **Hand off at about 120k context.** Set by the owner on 2026-10-02 after
  usage showed 66% of tokens spent above 150k context and 67% in implementer
  subagents. An implementer that nears 120k commits what is safe, writes its
  handoff and stops, even mid-phase; a fresh agent continues from the handoff.
  The coordinator plans phases so that none is expected to reach that size,
  splitting a phase into sub-phases (2a, 2b) rather than letting one run long.
- **Backend tests: focused per phase, full once per slice** (OPS-01,
  `backend/TESTING.md`). Each phase runs the focused and adjacent backend files
  it touched; the real-Postgres full suite runs once per slice, on the final
  revision, with the guide's normal command and JUnit zero-skip check.
  Reviewers (Claude or Codex) cite that same-revision full evidence and run
  focused files; they rerun the full suite only for a finding that needs it.
  Before any full run start `tools/keep-awake-during-tests.ps1` and cap the run
  at twice its expected wall time; a run past the cap is stopped and reported,
  never waited on. One full suite on this machine at a time.
- **Mechanical sub-steps run on Sonnet.** Work fixed by an agreed contract or
  handoff - tests written against a settled interface, an AC-by-AC evidence
  pass, closeout records - is dispatched with `model: sonnet`, like fix rounds.
  Steps that decide a design stay on the session model.

## Token Efficiency Without Lowering Quality (owner, 2026-10-18)

Owner: be very efficient without compromising quality, in every session. Lessons
from W1 rework and W2-00..W2-03 (about 30-40% avoidable rework, then 15-20%):

- **All acceptance rules in the brief from the start.** Layout contract,
  recurring-defect checklist, height rule, plain-language rules, proof list.
  Never introduce a rule mid-build; it forces trimming passes on every slice.
- **Build specs answer the known question classes before coding.** A W2+ build
  spec is ready only when it fixes, against the actual code: identifier
  grammars and formats; exact fixture Site/Foundation values and how bindings
  are published; every seam with path:symbol; guard allowlists the new code
  must satisfy (rule 8 writers, sqlalchemy import allowlist, dependency
  direction); refusal codes; freshness/time references; UI empty/unavailable
  copy and the panel map; how Lab/run projections and stored artifacts look.
  W2-02 needed six question rounds because its first spec skipped these.
  Then the Architect does an **implementer dry-read**: walk the spec commit by
  commit against the real code, write down every question a mid-level
  implementer would have to ask (multiplicity such as per-connector channels,
  which component owns each device, new Foundation keys, readiness codes for
  each missing input, cadence sources, fixture Sites vs revisions, migration
  numbering against in-flight slices), and answer each in the spec. W2-05a
  stopped with eight such questions after the gate alone.
- **Size builder runs to one spec commit each**, with a handoff after each, so
  no run dies mid-commit and the next run reads only the handoff.
- **Model by risk.** First review of a new build and design-deciding work on
  the session model; fix rounds, re-reviews (scoped to prior findings) and
  mechanical steps on Sonnet. A capture- or copy-only fix may be verified by
  the coordinator directly (view the evidence, record a short verify note).
- **Serialize slices that edit the same shared UI files**; parallelize only
  disjoint work (backend vs frontend, different domains).
- **One full suite per round, only when no builder is running.** Do not rerun
  full suites after doc-, test- or CSS-only changes; rerun the touched files.
- **Batch Architect/Planner questions into one Codex call**, and fold a
  preemptive sweep of the slice's remaining commits into that same call.
- **No duplicate agents.** Check for a live agent on a worktree before
  dispatching; never reuse a worktree a reviewer is still using.
- **Mockup drives the design; implementers never decide (owner, 2026-10-18).**
  Every spec maps each mock panel, row, column and chip of each owned screen to
  its data source or an honest gap; no mock element is trimmed or redesigned to
  fit the backend. Any implementer choice the spec did not fix is a defect:
  reviewers flag it Medium (High if user-visible vs the mock), and the Architect
  rules it and amends the spec before merge
  (`.agent/owner-fidelity-and-design-ownership-2026-10-18.md`).
- **Briefs point to files; they do not restate them.** Coordinator replies to
  interim agent notifications stay to one line.

## Updating Bindings

Change bindings here when the team wants a different tool to own a role. Record
material changes in `.ai/DECISIONS.md` if they affect workflow, review
independence, or task ownership.

# W2-05a code-anchored build spec

Decision `D-2026-10-18-w2-05-buildspec`. Implements `tasks/W2-05a-ev-foundation.md` against current main after merged W2-00..03; the build branch must receive W2-04's `0023` before creating `0024`. Authority: amended `.agent/W2-technical-design-ev.md`, `D-2026-10-18-w2-mockup-authority`, `D-2026-10-17-mockup-layout-spec`. EV mock content and layout are binding. The cited symbols, rather than older design line numbers, are current code anchors.

## Architect dry-read resolution (2026-10-18; governs any earlier ambiguous wording)

**QA-QD amendment (2026-10-18; governs the W2-05a commit-1 handoff).**

- **QA, timezone:** Use `Etc/UTC` as the EV-LAB-001 Site timezone and as both schedule `time_zone` values, with `utc_offset_minutes=0`. It is an IANA Area/Location name accepted by the shared Site parser. Do not widen that parser for bare `UTC`; replace the earlier fixture wording `UTC` wherever it denotes a Site or schedule zone. UTC timestamps and offset-zero semantics are unchanged.
- **QB, property kinds:** `ev-charger-max-power`, `ev-connector-max-power`, `ev-meter-resolution`, `ev-meter-error-bound` and `ev-clock-error-bound` are `PHYSICAL`. `ev-site-import-limit`, `ev-status-cadence` and `ev-meter-cadence` are `CONTROL`. `ev-connector-enabled` remains `CONTROL`. These are definition kinds, independent of whether a value is currently observed.
- **QC, absent feed:** A Site with no `AC_BUS` may publish a declared charger, meter and connector as an incomplete configuration, provided all declared charger/connector properties and schedule entries are valid. More than one parent for a meter, charger or connector always refuses publication. Once a feed is declared, every declared meter must have exactly that feed as its parent and every declared charger must have exactly one path through a meter from that feed; an unfed meter or charger refuses `EV_CONFIGURATION_INVALID`. Any nonempty connector service schedule requires a complete feedâ†’meterâ†’chargerâ†’connector path, even when the Site has no feed.
- **QD, feed cardinality:** Refuse a second `AC_BUS` in an EV Foundation with `EV_CONFIGURATION_INVALID` and a path to the extra feed. The selected response has one `feed`, and no arbitrary feed selection or merged limit is valid. Zero or one feed is publishable subject to the rules above; Draft still requires the feed and its limit.

**QE-QG amendment (2026-10-18; governs the W2-05a commit-2 handoff).**

- **QE, Site import cadence:** `ev_site_import` binds the Site meter, but its `cadence_seconds` is the minimum of the selected Foundation revision's `ev-meter-cadence` values across all declared EV connectors, converted from minutes to seconds. This is the strictest configured maximum and is independent of binding request order or which connector reports. A Site with no declared connector cannot publish an EV binding set, so Site import cadence is never null on a published binding. Other meter-cadence roles use their bound connector's value.
- **QF, legacy EV channels:** Confirmed. Keep the two unsuffixed W2-00 reserved tuples and their EV role labels in the global v4 table for existing evidence tests, but exclude them from the closed `simulator-lab-gateway-ev/1` profile. `ev-roles-1` publication must refuse either tuple with `BINDING_CHANNEL_V4_UNKNOWN` for that profile; a role label in the global table alone does not authorize binding. The EV fixture and 05b producer use only the exact c1/c2 tuples and Site import tuple.
- **QG, source ownership:** Yes. In `validate_bindings`, for `ev-roles-1` refuse a request when the registered `SourceRow.asset_id` for its `device_id` differs from the requested `BindingRequest.asset_id`, using `BINDING_SOURCE_ASSET_MISMATCH` on that binding. This includes a c1 device bound to the c2 connector and a Site meter device bound to a different meter. Compare registered UUIDs, not device-name prefixes; keep the existing role/asset-type checks and S2 envelope-time `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH` for their separate purposes. A failed publication writes no binding revision.

**QH-QL amendment (2026-10-18; governs the W2-05a commit-3 handoff).**

- **QH, selected schedule:** Confirm `enabled_schedule` is always `{enabled,time_zone,weekly}` for each declared connector. `enabled` is true only for that connector's selected `ev-connector-enabled=ENABLED`; `time_zone` and `weekly` come from its entry in the same effective Foundation revision's `connector_enable_schedules`. Return an empty `weekly` array when there are no service hours, including a disabled connector. The UI must not equate enabled or scheduled with observed availability.
- **QI, clock bound:** Serve selected `ev-clock-error-bound` as `clock_error_bound_seconds` on each connector, a JSON decimal string. It is mandatory for every declared connector at publication, so null is only an absent connector/selection, never a default tolerance. In `ev-readiness` panel 2's existing **Clock and delivery** metric show `Configured clock error bound: {value} s` (per connector when values differ) as declared configuration; the observed clock-sync and delivery fields remain `Not assessed`. In `ev-foundation` panel 2, the **Connector status** row may refer to this configured tolerance in its supporting detail, without adding a row or asserting synchronized clocks. `ev-sld` has no clock slot and makes no clock claim.
- **QJ, feed shape:** Confirm `feed` is always an object `{asset_id,limit_kw,meter_asset_id}`; for no declared feed all three are null. If a feed exists, `meter_asset_id` is its sole directly supplied METER asset, else null. Do not pick one of multiple meters, use a connector meter as a substitute, or infer an import meter from a binding. The AC feed and meter diagram slots show `Not declared`/unknown when missing; charger/connector declarations remain visible. Multiple feed parents or a second feed still refuse publication.
- **QK, contact notice:** Emit `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED` only for `contact.state=NEVER`, including an explicitly unavailable diagnostic/match represented as NEVER with a reason. `STALE` is shown as stale gateway contact in `ev-readiness` panel 2 and does not receive this code; it does not become recent or imply charger failure. `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED` remains nonblocking and does not change `READY_TO_REPORT`.
- **QL, two times:** `at` selects the effective Foundation and binding revisions; it does not date the operational contact check. Read S7 gateway contact at request time, using the server's real UTC `read_at`/receipt freshness, and include `contact.checked_at` (UTC) so the UI labels the status **Contact checked {time}**. The latest eligible diagnostic is at or before that check, not constrained to selected `at`. A historical `selected_at` must not turn today's receipt into historical contact or make an old receipt look current. W2-03's window-end freshness uses its assessment window end because it judges evidence for that window; this Foundation route has no assessment window. `ev-readiness` panel 2 labels the selected configuration time separately from contact check time. `ev-foundation` and `ev-sld` show selected configuration only, not an event-window reporting claim.

Build in reviewable commits: **1 vocabulary, parser, typed schedule and validator; 2 EV roles, closed v4 channel matrix, binding publication/snapshot and fixture; 3 selected API; 4 three UI routes and visual proof; 5 migration and final guards/tests.** Commit 5 may be prepared only after W2-04 `0023` is merged into this branch. No interim EV migration is permitted; `0024_w2_ev_config` is reserved for 05a. Run each commit's focused checks and hand off before the next builder run.

**Q1, connector identity.** `BINDING_CHANNEL_OVERLAP` and `EV_CONNECTOR_MISMATCH` stay unchanged. In EV-LAB-001 the c1 tuples are `(c1-charger-controller,session-event)`, `(c1-connector-controller,fault-event|state|requested-power|allocated-limit)`, `(c1-connector-meter,energy|power)`; c2 uses the identical signal names with `c2-` device prefixes. Each of these three devices and seven channels per connector has `component_id=c1` or `c2` respectively and binds to that same EV_CONNECTOR asset. `(site-meter,import)` has `component_id=site-meter-1`, binds to its METER asset, and is the sole Site import channel. `charger-1` is topology and ratings only; it owns no connector channel. Each tuple is a distinct exact entry in `V4_CHANNEL_MAPPINGS`; no prefix wildcard or runtime-generated accepted tuple. Keep the two old unsuffixed W2-00 reserved tuples for their existing tests, label their roles, but do not bind them in this fixture. The v4 snapshot metadata must come from the exact entry. S2's existing one-binding connector check continues to compare the envelope connector asset ID to c1 or c2.

**Q2, readiness.** `CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE` applies to `CONNECTOR_NOT_DECLARED`, `FEED_OR_LIMIT_MISSING`, `METER_NOT_BOUND`, `SESSION_EVENTS_NOT_BOUND`, or `STATUS_NOT_BOUND`; `SITE_IMPORT_NOT_BOUND` is a nonblocking capability message under `READY_TO_REPORT`, as are `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED` and `COMMISSIONING_UNCONFIRMED`. Missing optional fault, connector power, requested power or allocated limit yields no readiness code; the exact capability that needs it stays unavailable. Required energy, event and state bindings are per declared connector. A configured Site may be ready while source contact and commissioning remain unknown.

**Q3, absent properties.** Missing `ev-site-import-limit` on an otherwise valid feed is the sole publishable numeric gap and emits `FEED_OR_LIMIT_MISSING`; absence of feed or all connectors is likewise publishable as configuration-incomplete so the selected read can explain it. For every *declared* charger/connector, missing `ev-charger-max-power`, `ev-connector-max-power`, `ev-connector-enabled`, `ev-meter-resolution`, `ev-meter-error-bound`, `ev-status-cadence`, `ev-meter-cadence` or `ev-clock-error-bound` refuses Foundation publication with `EV_CONFIGURATION_INVALID` and a path to that property. Present wrong type/unit, nonfinite/out-of-range value, invalid topology or schedule uses the same typed refusal. Do not pretend a missing physical or clock parameter is a readiness finding. A Site with no declared connector cannot publish a binding set or Draft a Lab run.

**Q4, cadence.** `ev-status-cadence` on each connector converts min to seconds and must be `0 < value <= 1`; it is the published state-channel cadence. `ev-meter-cadence` converts min to seconds and must be `0 < value <= 5`; it governs connector energy, connector power, Site import, requested power and allocated limit, all of which publish aligned 300-second *maximum* slots for this profile; the fixture declares exactly 5 min. Event channels have null cadence. `BindingRequest` has no cadence field: derive `bindings[].cadence_seconds` only from the selected Foundation property. Reject a property above its ceiling at Foundation publication (`EV_CONFIGURATION_INVALID`); reject any future submitted cadence field as `BINDING_REQUEST_INVALID`, not an invented `BINDING_CADENCE_INVALID`. `ev-clock-error-bound` is positive seconds and is recorded as the selected configured tolerance; 05a makes no observed clock-attestation claim. No cadence is inferred from observed timestamps.

**Q5, schedule.** Add `connector_enable_schedules` to `foundation_parsing.py:FOUNDATION_CONTENT_KEYS` and both parser allowlists through that shared constant; it is not `site_controls`. Exactly one schedule entry per declared connector, including a disabled connector (which has an empty `weekly` array); an enabled connector may have an empty weekly array, meaning no service hours. Each entry's zone equals the Site's IANA `timezone`. The fixture zone is `Etc/UTC`, weekly TUESDAY 08:00â€“16:00 with offset 0 for both c1/c2, so it recurs on Tuesdays during the revision's effective period, not just 6 October. For a bounded effective interval, enumerate each occurrence and validate offset and real, unambiguous local endpoints throughout that interval. For an open-ended interval, permit only zones whose offset is fixed for all future occurrences (`Etc/UTC` qualifies); a DST zone needs a bounded revision and a successor when its offset changes. Reject overlap, invalid endpoint and missing entry as `EV_CONFIGURATION_INVALID`. At selected `at`, the effective revision supplies its own schedule; never merge schedules across revisions.

**Q6, fixtures.** 05a creates `backend/tests/fixtures/w2/ev-lab-001.yaml` and registers the Site in its focused test/setup path. There is one Site, `EV-LAB-001`: the stocked Foundation and published binding revision are its first complete basis. Missing meter/event/status examples are separate *test-created binding revisions* on that same Site, with nonoverlapping effective periods after the 6 October complete-run window; the missing-limit example is a later Foundation revision on the same Site. Tests use isolated stores/DBs and pin `at` and revision IDs, so 05b's complete 6 October basis is untouched. No new Site codes are introduced. A revision never rewrites the original. 05b adds scenarios and uses this fixture, not a second copy.

**Q7, migration order.** W2-04 owns `0023`; 05a reserves `0024_w2_ev_config` now. Vocabulary/API/UI commits may proceed before 0023, but do not generate or test 0024 against 0022. Merge reviewed 0023 into the 05a branch, create `0024` with `down_revision` pointing to 0023, then run upgrade/downgrade and final tests. 05b adds no migration.

**Q8, kinds and profile.** `ev_connector_energy` has role `value_kind=INTERVAL_DELTA`, source/canonical unit kWh, parameter-free `IDENTITY_KWH` that accepts only kWh. Other EV interval roles use `value_kind=NUMBER`, `IDENTITY`, kW and v4 `INTERVAL_MEAN`; STATE uses `ENUM`, and EVENT uses `IDENTITY`. `(cX-connector-meter,energy)` requires nonnull session and connector in v4; power and controller interval channels require connector and carry session when a session exists; Site import has explicit-null session/connector. Preserve existing `V4_MAPPING_PROFILE_ID=simulator-lab-gateway-cold` for cold and its historical snapshots; EV binds under the new exact `simulator-lab-gateway-ev/1` profile, with an explicit profile-to-closed-table lookup. Do not mutate cold profile/version or v3 bytes. 05b registers the producer for that EV profile.

## Code seams and exact Foundation

Extend `backend/assetops_backend/sites/models.py:SITE_TYPES,COMPONENT_TYPES,COMPONENT_PROPERTY_DEFINITIONS,TOPOLOGY_NODE_ROLES,PROPERTY_UNITS`; `foundation_parsing.py:UNIT_RANGES` and its closed parser; `site_parsing.py` Site-type dispatch; `foundation_service.py` validation before publish. Follow `cold_foundation.py:validate_cold_foundation` with a pure `sites/ev_foundation.py:validate_ev_foundation` and selected projection, not a second persistence path. Add `EV_CHARGING`, `EV_CHARGER`, `EV_CONNECTOR`. Use zero or one existing `AC_BUS` feed, `METER` import meter, `EV_CHARGER` and `EV_CONNECTOR` distinct component IDs; a second feed refuses publication. `TOPOLOGY_NODE_ROLES` gains `CHARGER` and `CONNECTOR`; positions are `AC_BUS:BUS`, `METER:METERING`, charger `CHARGER`, connector `CONNECTOR`. Directed `topology.connections` uses existing `AC` medium for feedâ†’meterâ†’charger and `SIGNAL` for chargerâ†’connector; no parallel relation list. Refuse duplicate directed edges, cycles, orphan connector, more than one parent meter/charger/connector, and cross-Site links. With a declared feed, every meter and charger needs its full feedâ†’meterâ†’charger path; without a feed, an unfed meter or charger is publishable only as incomplete configuration and no connector may have scheduled service hours. No stationary battery is implied.

Define properties and carriers exactly: `ev-site-import-limit` kW on `AC_BUS` (CONTROL); `ev-charger-max-power` kW on `EV_CHARGER` (PHYSICAL); `ev-connector-max-power` kW (PHYSICAL) and `ev-connector-enabled` state (CONTROL) on `EV_CONNECTOR`; `ev-meter-resolution` and `ev-meter-error-bound` kWh on `EV_CONNECTOR` (PHYSICAL); `ev-status-cadence` and `ev-meter-cadence` min on `EV_CONNECTOR` (CONTROL), plus `ev-clock-error-bound` s on `EV_CONNECTOR` (PHYSICAL). Current `models.py:ComponentProperty.value` is numeric: extend that typed value to number-or-state and make `foundation_parsing.py:parse_component_properties,render_component_properties` branch only for `ev-connector-enabled`. Its value is the exact string `ENABLED|DISABLED`, unit `state`, kind CONTROL; all preexisting numeric properties keep identical parser/rendered bytes. The first three limits, meter resolution/cadences and clock bound are strictly positive finite values; meter error bound nonnegative finite. The selected fixture has feed limit 80 kW, two connector/charger ratings 60 kW, resolution/error bound 0.001 kWh, status cadence 1 min, meter cadence 5 min and clock error bound 30 s. A real Site must author these values; the pack has no silent production default. Schema change `0024_w2_ev_config` follows `0023`, adds the partial `(site_id,session_id,connector_id,source_time,id)` EV measurement index, retains existing Foundation history and 0020 family CHECK. Downgrade refuses with EV measurements, then drops the index while retaining historical Foundation reader safety. No new configuration SQL table.

Represent the connector enable schedule as a typed, time-valid Foundation control named `connector_enable_schedules`, an array of `{connector_asset_id,time_zone,weekly:[{weekday,local_start,local_end,utc_offset_minutes}]}`. `weekday` is uppercase MONDAYâ€¦SUNDAY, local times are `HH:MM`, intervals are half-open within one local day, `time_zone` is the Site IANA zone, and the explicit offset must match that zone for every occurrence in the Foundation revision's effective date range; publish a new revision when the offset changes. The fixture declares both connectors enabled TUESDAY 08:00â€“16:00, UTC offset 0, for 2026-10-06. Reject overlap, reversed/zero interval, ambiguous/nonexistent local instant, disabled connector marked available, and enablement without feed/meter/charger. Selected `at` uses the effective Foundation and binding revision at that instant; a missing selector uses the existing current-selection rule. Older revisions remain selectable and never inherit new limits/schedule. A configured service window is not observed availability.

## Role set, binding and selected read

Current `binding_roles.py:Role.value_kind` and its conversion validator accept NUMBER/STATE/EVENT, while `CONVERSION_KINDS` is IDENTITY/ENUM. Extend the closed validation to `INTERVAL_DELTA` and `IDENTITY_KWH` only for the `ev_connector_energy` role, preserving old role/conversion behavior and bytes. The W2-00 `(charger-controller,session-event)` and `(connector-controller,fault-event)` tuples already exist with no EV role label; attach their exact EV role labels and add the remaining EV tuples rather than duplicating those keys. The mapping's `requires_session`/connector rules remain the S2 authority.

Extend `sites/binding_roles.py:Role,ROLE_SET_BY_SITE_TYPE,roles_for` with immutable `ev-roles-1`. `ev_session_event` and optional `ev_connector_fault` are EVENT/`event`/IDENTITY per EV_CONNECTOR, one channel each; event kinds and outcomes are the W2-00 v4 allowlist. `ev_connector_state` is STATE/`state`/ENUM per connector, max 60-second cadence with `AVAILABLE|PLUGGED|AUTHORIZING|CHARGING|FAULTED|DISABLED`. `ev_connector_energy` is `INTERVAL_DELTA`/kWh/IDENTITY_KWH per connector, aligned 300-second half-open slots; `ev_connector_power` is optional `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW. `ev_site_import` is `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW ONE per feed and is required only for site-power comparison, not outcome/energy. `ev_requested_power` and `ev_allocated_limit` are optional independent `INTERVAL_MEAN`/kW controller channels. Outcome requires session events; fault association requires fault event; supported energy requires energy meter; service-state context requires status. Use `binding_service.py:validate_bindings,publish,acquisition_snapshot` and `binding_alignment.py` to bind exact registered source/device/signal, asset, unit, conversion, clock/cadence and effective interval. Add only the EV tuples to the closed `contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope_v4.py:V4_CHANNEL_MAPPINGS` table; `binding_service.py` supplies the five snapshot metadata keys at a new published revision, following W2-02, not on a historical conversion. Do not let one physical signal silently satisfy incompatible roles. Keep v3 bytes and cold binding snapshots unchanged.

`sites/ev_foundation.py` returns `readiness.state=READY_TO_REPORT|CONFIGURATION_INCOMPLETE` and only the design's eight codes: `CONNECTOR_NOT_DECLARED`, `FEED_OR_LIMIT_MISSING`, `METER_NOT_BOUND`, `SESSION_EVENTS_NOT_BOUND`, `STATUS_NOT_BOUND`, `SITE_IMPORT_NOT_BOUND`, `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED`, `COMMISSIONING_UNCONFIRMED`. The first six indicate configuration/capability; the last two are separate contact and commissioning notices. The registered Lab Draft gate in `binding_service.py:acquisition_snapshot` must use the selected EV rule rather than mini-grid electrical gaps: block absent connector/feed/limit or required session/status/energy bindings; optional site import/fault/power roles do not block a run that does not claim their capability. A wrong role set or failed effective binding refuses Draft. No test-only gap lift.

Add selected `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/ev/foundation?at=<UTC>` beside the existing cold Foundation router/composition (`rg 'build_cold_foundation_router' backend/assetops_backend`). Response: `site_id,site_code,site_type,selected_at,foundation_revision_id,binding_revision_id,feed:{asset_id,limit_kw,meter_asset_id},chargers:[{asset_id,max_kw,connectors:[{asset_id,max_kw,enabled_schedule:{enabled,time_zone,weekly},meter_resolution_kwh,meter_error_bound_kwh,clock_error_bound_seconds}]}],topology:{nodes,edges},bindings:[{role,asset_id,source_id,device_id,signal_id,unit,cadence_seconds,effective_from,effective_to}],readiness:{state,messages},contact:{state,as_of,checked_at,gateway_id,reason},commissioning:{state,evidence_ids}`. `feed` is present even without a feed, with three null fields; its meter ID is the sole direct meter or null. JSON decimal quantities are decimal strings; unknowns are null, not zero. Use the cold selected-read `Access`/source gate: 401 `UNAUTHENTICATED`, 403 `SITE_SCOPE_FORBIDDEN`, 404 `SITE_NOT_FOUND` including wrong type after access check, 422 `EV_SELECTION_INVALID`, 503 `EV_FOUNDATION_UNAVAILABLE`; malformed/extra query fields refuse. `contact` maps S7 CURRENTâ†’RECENT, STALEâ†’STALE, absent diagnosticâ†’NEVER with reason and null `as_of`; selected gateway identity comes from registration, not mock GW-EV-01. Per-source contact is unavailable in 05a. `commissioning` is always `UNCONFIRMED`, `evidence_ids=[]` until a real record exists; do not manufacture it from a good gateway receipt. Contact recency is measured at server request time (`checked_at`), separately from configuration selection (`selected_at`), and the UI states that it is gateway contact, not charger health.

.agent/W2-technical-design-ev.md:42:**Discrete model.** One-minute UTC step, exact decimal arithmetic; default fixture has two 60 kW connectors, 80 kW site import cap, both enabled [08:00,16:00) UTC on 2026-10-06, site non-EV base load 0 kW. State per connector: `UNPLUGGED|PLUGGED|AUTHORIZING|CHARGING|FAULTED`, session ID or null, authorization result, requested kW, allocated kW, meter accumulator kWh, fault latch. Private forcing: plug/unplug, authorization accept/deny, request schedule, fault/clear, grid interruption, publication dropout/latency. Initial unplugged, no session/fault, accumulator 0. At each minute: apply forcing ordered `(time,connector,kind)`; a plug assigns next deterministic scoped session ID; authorization takes 1 minute; acceptance emits START when CHARGING begins; denial produces no START and is a contextual observed state, not a failed session. Allocate shared available power `max(0,site_limit-base_load)` by connector ID ascending in round-robin 1 kW quanta among charging connectors up to `min(request,connector_limit,charger_limit)`; persist round-robin pointer so no connector monopolizes successive minutes. `E_next=E+allocated_kW/60`, exact kWh. A fault latches FAULTED, emits FAULT and ABORT for an active started session; explicit user unplug emits END (completed) only after active charge and no fault; grid interruption pauses allocation and connector state, it does not invent ABORT. Fault clear requires explicit forcing and emits CLEAR; a new plug creates a new session. Simultaneous END/ABORT or START twice for one ID is prohibited by simulator sanity guard. Fail run on negative energy, allocated sum above available/site/connector limits, energy residual, state with no valid session, duplicate conflicting forcing or non-monotone time.

const ST={good:['#2f8f6f','#2f8f6f'],gap:['#d6a052','#a46008'],none:['#fff','#8a9bb3']};
function stDot(x,y,st){const [f,c]=ST[st]||ST.none;return `<circle cx="${x}" cy="${y}" r="5.5" fill="${f}" stroke="${c}" stroke-width="1.6"${st==='none'?' stroke-dasharray="2 2"':''}/>`;}
function brk(x,y,label){return `<circle cx="${x-11}" cy="${y}" r="3.2" class="sld-sym"/><circle cx="${x+11}" cy="${y}" r="3.2" class="sld-sym"/><path d="M${x-11} ${y-3}Q${x} ${y-15} ${x+11} ${y-3}" class="sld-sym"/>${label?`<text x="${x}" y="${y-19}" text-anchor="middle" class="sld-small">${label}</text>`:''}`;}
function srcIcon(kind,x,y){if(kind==='pv')return `<rect x="${x}" y="${y}" width="96" height="52" rx="4" class="sld-box pv"/>${[0,1,2].map(c=>[0,1].map(r=>`<rect x="${x+8+c*28}" y="${y+7+r*20}" width="24" height="16" class="sld-cell"/>`).join('')).join('')}`;
 if(kind==='gen')return `<rect x="${x}" y="${y}" width="96" height="52" rx="4" class="sld-box gen"/><circle cx="${x+30}" cy="${y+26}" r="15" class="sld-gen"/><text x="${x+30}" y="${y+31}" text-anchor="middle" class="sld-glyph">G</text><rect x="${x+54}" y="${y+14}" width="30" height="24" rx="2" class="sld-gen"/>`;
 if(kind==='bat')return `<rect x="${x}" y="${y}" width="96" height="52" rx="4" class="sld-box bat"/>${[0,1,2,3].map(c=>`<rect x="${x+10+c*20}" y="${y+12}" width="14" height="28" rx="2" class="sld-batcell"/>`).join('')}`;
 return `<rect x="${x}" y="${y}" width="96" height="52" rx="4" class="sld-box grid"/><path d="M${x+48} ${y+6}L${x+34} ${y+46}M${x+48} ${y+6}L${x+62} ${y+46}M${x+38} ${y+22}H${x+58}M${x+36} ${y+34}H${x+60}" class="sld-sym"/>`;}
function sld(domain){const d=SLD[domain],W=1000,rowS=100,rowF=86,top=46;
 const nS=d.sources.length,nF=d.feeders.length,busTop=top-6,busBot=top+Math.max(nS*rowS,nF*rowF)-30,monY=busBot+52,H=monY+70;

  else g+=stDot(112,y+4,src.st);
  const grp=(d.groups||[]).find(G=>G.rows.includes(i));

  else{g+=`<line x1="${x}" x2="${busX}" y1="${cy}" y2="${cy}" class="sld-ac"/>`+brk(570,cy,src.cb);}
 });

 * "Three routes and fidelity proof"; `D-2026-10-17-mockup-layout-spec`). Configuration only: a configured

/* Frame -------------------------------------------------------------------- */

export interface EvFoundationFrameProps {
  view: FoundationView;
  siteId: string;
  siteName: string;
  mode: string | null;
  selectedAt: string | null;
  onSelectAt: (at: string | null) => void;
  client: EvFoundationClient;
  gatewayHealth?: GatewayHealthClient;
  versions: FoundationVersionsReadModel | null;
  viewHref: (target: FoundationView) => string;
  evidenceHref: string;
  healthHref: string;
  financialsHref: string;
  assumptionsHref: string;
  tabs: ReactNode;
  parentTrail: Crumb[];
}

const DESCRIPTION: Record<FoundationView, string> = {
  model: SITE_SCREEN_DESCRIPTIONS["ev-foundation"],
  readiness: SITE_SCREEN_DESCRIPTIONS["ev-readiness"],
  sld: SITE_SCREEN_DESCRIPTIONS["ev-sld"],
};

export function EvFoundationFrame(props: EvFoundationFrameProps) {
  const { view, siteId, siteName, selectedAt, client, gatewayHealth } = props;
  const [read, setRead] = useState<{ key: string; value: EvFoundationRead } | null>(null);
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0);
  const key = `${siteId}|${selectedAt ?? ""}|${attempt}`;
  useEffect(() => {
    let active = true;
    void client.getEvFoundation(siteId, selectedAt).then((value) => {
      if (active) setRead({ key, value });
    });
    return () => {
      active = false;
    };
  }, [client, siteId, selectedAt, key]);
  const retry = useCallback(() => setAttempt((n) => n + 1), []);

  const [health, setHealth] = useState<EvidenceRead<GatewayHealth> | null>(null);
  useEffect(() => {
    if (gatewayHealth === undefined) {
      setHealth({ status: "unavailable", message: null });
      return undefined;
    }
    let active = true;
    void gatewayHealth.getGatewayHealth(siteId).then((value) => {
      if (active) setHealth(value);
    });
    return () => {
      active = false;
    };
  }, [gatewayHealth, siteId]);

  const current = read !== null && read.key === key ? read.value : null;
  const trail: Crumb[] =
    view === "model"
      ? [...props.parentTrail, { label: "Foundation" }]
      : [...props.parentTrail, { label: "Foundation", to: props.viewHref("model") }, { label: foundationViewLabel(view) }];

  return (
    <main aria-labelledby={EV_HEADING_ID} data-ev-foundation-view={view}>
      <Breadcrumbs trail={trail} />
      <SitePageHeader siteName={siteName} siteId={siteId} mode={props.mode} description={DESCRIPTION[view]} headingId={EV_HEADING_ID} />
      {props.tabs}
      <FoundationSubtabs current={view} hrefFor={props.viewHref} withChanges={false} />
      {current === null ? (
        <p>{EV_FOUNDATION_LOADING}</p>
      ) : current.status === "loaded" ? (
        <EvViews {...props} read={current.value} health={health} gateway={gatewayName(current.value, health)} />
      ) : (
        <EvRefusal {...props} read={current} onRetry={retry} />
      )}
    </main>
  );
}

/** The selected gateway's registered name from the health read, matched by the EV read's selected gateway. */
function gatewayName(read: EvFoundationReadModel, health: EvidenceRead<GatewayHealth> | null): string {
  if (read.contact.gateway_id === null) return NO_GATEWAY_SELECTED;
  if (health !== null && health.status === "loaded") {
    const match = health.value.gateways.find((g) => g.gateway_id === read.contact.gateway_id);
    if (match !== undefined) return match.gateway_external_id;
  }
  return GATEWAY_NOT_READ;
}

/* Empty, refused or unavailable read: the page-level state, then every slot with its gap. */

type RefusalKind = "empty" | "forbidden" | "unavailable";

function refusalKind(read: Exclude<EvFoundationRead, { status: "loaded" }>): RefusalKind {
  if (read.status === "refused" && read.code === "FOUNDATION_REVISION_NOT_FOUND") return "empty";
  if (read.status === "refused" && read.httpStatus === 403) return "forbidden";
  return "unavailable";
}

function EvRefusal(props: EvFoundationFrameProps & { read: Exclude<EvFoundationRead, { status: "loaded" }>; onRetry: () => void }) {
  const kind = refusalKind(props.read);
  const title = kind === "empty" ? EMPTY_SELECTION : kind === "forbidden" ? FORBIDDEN : UNAVAILABLE;
  const gap =
    kind === "empty"
      ? `${EMPTY_SELECTION}, so nothing is shown as configured in this panel.`
      : kind === "forbidden"
        ? `${FORBIDDEN}, so this panel shows no configuration.`
        : `${UNAVAILABLE}, so nothing is shown as configured in this panel. This is not an empty configuration.`;
  return (
    <>
      <div className="page-refusal" role="status" data-page-refusal={kind}>
        <p className="page-refusal__title">
          <strong>{title}</strong>
        </p>
        {kind === "empty" ? (
          <p>{`No EV configuration of this site is in effect at ${formatInstant(props.selectedAt)}. The configuration in effect now is not shown in its place.`}</p>
        ) : kind === "unavailable" ? (
          <p>The site's EV configuration could not be read. Nothing is shown as configured until it can be.</p>
        ) : null}
        {kind === "empty" && props.selectedAt !== null ? (
          <button type="button" className="button primary" onClick={() => props.onSelectAt(null)}>
            Show the version in effect now
          </button>
        ) : null}
        {kind === "unavailable" ? (
          <button type="button" className="button primary" onClick={props.onRetry}>
            Try again
          </button>
        ) : null}
        {kind === "forbidden" ? null : (
          <ExactReferences
            name="ev-refusal-references"
            variant="tech"
            references={[
              { label: "Refusal code", value: props.read.status === "refused" ? props.read.code : null },
              { label: "Selected time (exact)", value: props.selectedAt },
            ]}
          />
        )}
      </div>
      <EvSkeletons view={props.view} siteName={props.siteName} gateway={GATEWAY_NOT_READ} gap={gap} />
    </>
  );
}

{    "allowedAcronyms": ["SOC", "UTC", "kW", "kWh", "PV"],    "glosses": [
/**
 * The T028 product vocabulary guard (D-2026-10-04-plain-language-analysis-views,
 * `.agent/T028-architect-presentation.md` section 4).
 *
 * It scans what a screen actually says - every rendered text node, including
 * text a backend delivered, graphics' captions, legends and `<title>`s - and
 * every accessible label an assistive technology would read (`aria-label`,
 * `title`, `alt`, `placeholder`, `aria-description`), against the versioned
 * list in `productVocabulary.json`. Matching is on word boundaries, so `run`
 * does not match `truncate`, and a failure names the page and the phrase.
 *
 * Exclusions are only the three the decision names, applied to one element at
 * a time with `data-vocabulary-exempt="<reason>"`: an exact source-owned
 * reference value, a user-owned name, or a Lab entry. An exempt element must
 * wrap a single value - one with element children is itself a violation - so a
 * whole panel or a backend response cannot be exempted by marking its root.
 *
 * The guard is a regression aid. A person still has to judge plain meaning,
 * uncertainty and claim strength.
 */
import vocabulary from "./productVocabulary.json";

export interface VocabularyViolation {
  page: string;
  phrase: string;
  /** Where the phrase was found: `text` or the attribute name. */
  where: string;
  text: string;
}

const LABEL_ATTRIBUTES = [
  "aria-label",
  "aria-description",
  "aria-roledescription",
  "title",
  "alt",
  "placeholder",
] as const;

export const EXEMPT_ATTRIBUTE = "data-vocabulary-exempt";
/**
 * Exact source-owned reference values inside authored text, on the element
 * that renders them, separated by " | ". The scan removes exactly those values
 * from that element's text and labels and still scans the authored words
 * around them. A value must have the shape of an identifier (letters, digits,
 * `.`, `_`, `-`, joined by " / ", optionally " vN"), so prose cannot be
 * passed off as a reference.
 */
export const REFERENCE_ATTRIBUTE = "data-vocabulary-reference";
const REFERENCE_SHAPE = /^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+(?: \/ [A-Za-z0-9._-]+)*(?: v\d+)?$/;
export const VOCABULARY_VERSION: number = vocabulary.version;
export const EXEMPTION_REASONS = Object.keys(vocabulary.exemptions);

interface Term {
  phrase: string;
  pattern: RegExp;
}

/** A `code` term also treats `_` as part of a word, so `PENDING` is not found inside `PENDING_REASSESSMENT`. */
function phraseTerm(phrase: string, caseSensitive: boolean, code = false): Term {
  const word = code ? "\\p{L}\\p{N}_" : "\\p{L}\\p{N}";
  return {
    phrase,
    pattern: new RegExp(
      `(?<![${word}])${escape(phrase).replace(/ /g, "\\s+")}(?![${word}])`,
      caseSensitive ? "u" : "iu",
    ),
  };
}

const TERMS: Term[] = vocabulary.terms.map((term) =>
  phraseTerm(term.phrase, "caseSensitive" in term && term.caseSensitive === true),
);

const GLOSSES = vocabulary.glosses.map(
  (gloss) => new RegExp(escape(gloss.text), "giu"),
);

function escape(text: string): string {
  return text.replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, "\\$&");
}

/**
 * The W1 set (D-2026-10-16-w1-ux, D-2026-10-16-w1-ux-disclosure), versioned
 * separately inside the same file. A route opts in as its W1 slice migrates
 * it; the T028 list keeps applying to every route either way.
 *
 * Raw codes are matched case-sensitively: a closed list of single-word state
 * codes, plus shape patterns for uppercase underscore tokens, UUIDs, digests
 * and rule/model versions. Allow-listed units and acronyms (SOC, UTC, kW, kWh,
 * PV) are never reported as raw codes; the T028 gloss rule for acronyms is a
 * separate rule and still applies.
 */
export const W1_VOCABULARY_VERSION: number = vocabulary.w1.version;
const W1_ALLOWED = new Set<string>(vocabulary.w1.allowedAcronyms);
const W1_TERMS: Term[] = vocabulary.w1.terms.map((term) => phraseTerm(term.phrase, false));
const W1_CODES: Term[] = vocabulary.w1.codes.map((code) => phraseTerm(code, true, true));
const W1_PATTERNS = vocabulary.w1.patterns.map((p) => ({ name: p.name, pattern: new RegExp(p.source, `${p.flags}g`) }));
const W1_GLOSSES = vocabulary.w1.glosses.map((gloss) => new RegExp(escape(gloss.text), "giu"));
const DISCLOSURE_TERMS: Term[] = [
  ...vocabulary.w1.disclosure.phrases.map((phrase) => phraseTerm(phrase, false)),
  ...vocabulary.w1.disclosure.words.map((word) => phraseTerm(word, false)),
  ...vocabulary.w1.disclosure.codes.map((code) => phraseTerm(code, true, true)),
];
const UNDERSCORE_CODE = W1_PATTERNS[0];

/** Which list a scan applies: the T028 list alone, or T028 plus the W1 set. */
export type VocabularySet = "t028" | "w1";

/**
 * The raw codes one piece of text contains: closed-list state codes and the
 * shape patterns, minus allow-listed units and acronyms. Case-sensitive, so
 * ordinary lower-case words ("current", "pending") are never codes.
 */
export function rawCodes(text: string): string[] {
  const found: string[] = [];
  for (const code of W1_CODES) if (code.pattern.test(text)) found.push(code.phrase);
  for (const { name, pattern } of W1_PATTERNS) {
    for (const match of text.matchAll(pattern)) {
      if (W1_ALLOWED.has(match[0])) continue;
      found.push(`${name}: ${match[0]}`);
    }
  }
  return found;
}

function withoutGlosses(text: string, glosses: RegExp[]): string {
  let scanned = text;
  for (const gloss of glosses) scanned = scanned.replace(gloss, " ");
  return scanned;

Bind every value to the selected read or show the named gap in the same cell. `ev-foundation` panel 1 keeps the mock **Site ID, Connectors, Site import limit, Stationary storage, Session source** rows: Site ID uses `site_code`; Connectors uses declared connector count/ratings and selected schedule without claiming service; import limit uses `feed.limit_kw` or `Site import limit not declared`; storage is `Not declared` absent a stored asset; Session source uses the session-event bindings or `Needs a source`. The selected feed, import meter and topology remain inspectable in panel 1 supporting detail, with no invented row. Panel 2 keeps the mock **Session identity mapping, Transaction completeness, Connector status, Revenue assumption** rows: identity mapping uses session-event bindings; completeness is `Not assessed` without accepted session assessment; status says `Mapped` only from state bindings and may show the configured clock bound as supporting detail, never observed sync; revenue assumption is `No scenario tariff in use` in 05a. Declared feed/charger/connector limits and schedule stay in contextual detail within these panels. No energy, charging-status observation, or live flow is inferred from a schedule.
87:`ev-readiness` panel 1 keeps the mock `Area | Declared | Confirmed | What is needed | Next step` table. Connectors/site limit derive from Foundation; status-point mapping derives from bindings; confirmation is `UNCONFIRMED` without a commissioning record. Billing integration and tariff rows remain, with `Not connected` / `No scenario tariff in use` because 05a has no billing or tariff read; their links are accessible unavailable entries until those routes exist. Panel 2 keeps the four mock metric slots and availability strip: **Status** and last gateway contact use S7 diagnostic at request-time `contact.checked_at`; **Availability** and **Data completeness** remain `Not assessed`; **Clock and delivery** shows configured connector clock error bound from `clock_error_bound_seconds` while observed sync and delivery speed remain `Not assessed`. Keep the panel subtitle's selected configuration `selected_at` distinct from `Contact checked {checked_at}`. Expected/received counts, late count, firmware and point-list version are `Not assessed` or `Source contact unavailable` in their respective slots. `STALE` is labelled stale contact without `SOURCE_NOT_CONTACTED`; `NEVER` shows `No gateway contact recorded` and the nonblocking notice. The strip uses unknown cells, never green uptime. Panel 3 keeps one connected-source chip per registered binding with role and source identity; per-source dot is unknown and its contact copy is `Source contact unavailable`. Panel 4 keeps both numbered attention rows; without accepted connector-hour and session assessment reads, each says `Not assessed` and points to available Evidence, with any unavailable Action shown as noninteractive `Not available yet`. Do not restate the mock's connector-2 silence or missing SES-012 end as facts.
89:`ev-sld` panel 1 retains the diagram nodes, lines, legend and status-dot locations. AC feed→meter→charger uses AC style; charger→connector control relation uses SIGNAL style. A missing feed or sole direct import meter leaves its original diagram slot `Not declared`/unknown while declared chargers and connectors remain visible. Breaker, depot auxiliary, DC conversion and charger-side reporting dots with no declared/accepted source remain visible as `Not declared` / unknown in the same slot, rather than invented equipment or a green dot. Panel 2 keeps `Part | Role at this site | What it reports`: feed+load manager, charger/connector rows, and depot auxiliary row; declare limits and binding roles where present, with `Function not recorded` or `Not declared` for the other cells. A state binding means mapped status, not proof of reporting; selected schedule never colors a reporting dot. Preserve the mock's footer and explanatory notice, replacing claims of reporting in an event window with the configured-vs-observed limitation. Navigation links to unbuilt routes remain accessible, noninteractive `Not available yet`.

Use the existing Foundation/readiness/SLD URLs; no new route hierarchy. Put feed/chargers/connectors/limits/bindings into their exact mock slots. No extra leading answer card, live flow, inferred battery, health badge or mock fixture number.
